[STEP 0] [USER] | 2026-10-26T19:10:34Z

Your chat history is very likely still intact on your hard drive. When Antigravity IDE crashes or closes unexpectedly while messages are queued, the actual conversation files (.pb or .db files) remain safe in your local directory. The reason the chat disappeared is that the unexpected shutdown caused a desynchronization in the trajectorySummaries index file, which tells the UI what chats to display. [1, 2, 3]
Here is how to recover your missing session step-by-step:
Method 1: The @ Search Shortcut (Quick Reference)
If you just need to access the context or reference the code from that specific lost chat right now: [4]
Open a New Chat inside Antigravity IDE.
Type @ in the message box and select the Conversations tab.
Look through the list or search keywords from your vanished chat to pull the data directly into your active workspace. [4]
Method 2: Rebuild the Index Database (Automated Re-sync)
You can force Antigravity IDE to rescrape your local directory and pull the vanished conversation back into your sidebar: [4]
Completely close Antigravity IDE and ensure no background instances are running in your Task Manager or Activity Monitor. [5]
Open your system runner (Win + R on Windows) or terminal, and navigate to your global storage directory:
Windows: %APPDATA%\Antigravity\User\globalStorage\
macOS: ~/Library/Application Support/Antigravity/User/globalStorage/ [4, 6]
Find the file named state.vscdb and make a backup copy of it somewhere safe just in case. [4]
Open your Terminal / Command Prompt and run the following command to delete the desynced index cache:sqlite3 "%APPDATA%\Antigravity\User\globalStorage\state.vscdb" "DELETE FROM ItemTable WHERE key='antigravityUnifiedStateSync.trajectorySummaries';"
(Note: Adjust the path if you are on Mac/Linux). [4]
Reopen the IDE. The language server will scan your folders and automatically rebuild your sidebar history. [4]
Method 3: Use the Community Recovery Tool
If manual database commands feel intimidating, the community developed an open-source, automated utility specifically for this exact bug. [7]
Close the IDE completely.
Download the Antigravity-Database-Manager utility (or the antigravity-conversation-fix script).
Run python antigravity_database_manager.py recover (or execute the run.bat package). It will automatically parse your raw .pb/.db conversation logs and push them back into your sidebar layout. [3, 5, 7, 8]
If your history still doesn't pop up after trying these steps, let me know:
What Operating System you are running (Windows, macOS, Linux)?
Have you recently updated to the Antigravity 2.0+ architecture split?
Do you see files inside your ~/.gemini/antigravity-ide/conversations folder? [9, 10]

The search for ""ag-donald/Antigravity-Database-Manager" "Quickstart" OR "python"" returned the following summary:
The **`ag-donald/Antigravity-Database-Manager`** is an unofficial community database manager and recovery tool designed for the Google Antigravity IDE[1]. It is primarily used to fix a common bug where conversation history disappears from the IDE's sidebar (often triggered by updates, restarts, or workspace shifts) by rebuilding the internal SQLite and JSON indices straight from your intact local `.pb` conversation files[2].

### Quickstart Guide

The tool is written in pure Python with **zero external dependencies** (relying entirely on the standard library)[1], making it straightforward to run locally.

1. **Important:** Close the Antigravity IDE completely before running any database modifications[2].
2. Run the script directly using Python[2]:
   ```bash
   python antigravity_database_manager.py
   ```
   *(Alternatively, if using the portable compiled release artifact provided in the repository, you can run: `python AgmerciumRecovery.pyz`)*[3][2]
3. Follow the interactive prompts in the terminal interface to scan, repair, or rebuild your indices, then reopen your IDE[2].

### Key Features
* **Zero Dependencies:** Requires only standard Python libraries (Python 3.10+)[1].
* **Backup-First Architecture:** Automatically creates timestamped backups before performing any write operations to local databases[4].
* **Multiple Interfaces:** Offers a full-screen Text User Interface (TUI), a headless interactive mode, and granular CLI subcommands[2] (such as handling Windows workspace URI encoding normalization via `fix-uris`)[3].

[STEP 29] [AI ASSISTANT] | 2026-10-26T19:12:17Z
~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~
Created At: 2026-10-27T00:42:17+05:30
Completed At: 2026-10-27T00:42:21+05:30
The search for "site:github.com/ag-donald/Antigravity-Database-Manager "antigravity_database_manager.py"" returned the following summary:
The **`antigravity_database_manager.py`** file is the main entry point for the [Antigravity-Database-Manager](https://github.com/ag-donald/Antigravity-Database-Manager) repository by `ag-donald`[1][2]. 

### Overview of the Tool
* **Purpose:** It is an unofficial, open-source community database manager and recovery tool built for the **Google Antigravity IDE** (an agent-first VS Code fork)[3][4]. 
* **Core Functionality:** It fixes issues where conversation history disappears from the IDE's UI sidebar (often triggered by IDE updates, unexpected shutdowns, or workspace transitions) by rebuilding internal SQLite indices (`state.vscdb`) from intact local Protobuf (`.pb`) and conversation files[3][4][5][6].
* **Key Characteristics:** 
  * **Zero Dependencies:** Written using only Python standard library modules[7].
  * **Cross-Platform:** Works on Windows, macOS, and Linux[8][9].
  * **Backup-First:** Automatically generates timestamped backups prior to making any modifications to local databases[7].

### Common Commands Using `antigravity_database_manager.py`

1. **Launch the Full-Screen TUI (Text User Interface):**
   ```bash
   python antigravity_database_manager.py
   ```
   *(Running without arguments starts the interactive management hub or falls back to headless interactive mode if stdout is not a TTY)[10].*

2. **Fix Workspace URI Encoding Issues (Bug #12 - Windows Drive-Letter Mismatch):**
   * Preview changes (dry run):
     ```bash
     python antigravity_database_manager.py fix-uris --dry-run
     ```
   * Patch the IDE bundle directly:
     ```bash
     python antigravity_database_manager.py fix-uris
     ```
   * Patch the IDE bundle **and** normalize URIs inside `state.vscdb`:
     ```bash
     python antigravity_database_manager.py fix-uris --db
     ```

3. **Database Maintenance & Recovery:**
   * Create an empty database template:
     ```bash
     python antigravity_database_manager.py create --output /path/to/new.vscdb
     ```
   * Merge or recover conversation entries from backups[11]:
     ```bash
     python antigravity_database_manager.py merge --source backup.vscdb
     ```

The search for ""FutureisinPast/antigravity-conversation-fix"" returned the following summary:
**`FutureisinPast/antigravity-conversation-fix`** is an open-source utility hosted on GitHub designed to resolve a common issue in **Google Antigravity** (and the Antigravity IDE) where chat and conversation history suddenly disappears, becomes unordered, or fails to load properly after updates or app crashes[1][2][3].

### What Problem Does It Solve?
* **Missing & Disappearing Chat History:** Due to updates, index resets, or internal workspace state corruption, Antigravity users frequently encounter situations where previous chat histories vanish from the sidebar or UI[4][3].
* **Unordered Conversations:** Fixes sorting and sequencing problems where message logs or conversation threads appear scattered or jumbled[1].
* **Cross-Platform Support:** Provides scripts/executables compatible with Windows, macOS, and Linux[2].

### How It Generally Works
1. **Safety First:** It scans your local Antigravity/IDE storage directories (where local workspace states and conversation data files are kept) and generates timestamped backups before touching anything[5].
2. **Rebuilding the Index:** It safely parses existing local conversation files/caches, rebuilds missing indices, and safely hooks them back into the application's storage so the UI can read them again[4].

### Basic Usage Steps
*In general, community tools of this nature require users to follow these steps:*
1. Completely close out of all running instances of Google Antigravity / Antigravity IDE (to prevent file-locking or data-overwriting issues)[2][3].
2. Download or run the provided script/executable from the official GitHub repository (`FutureisinPast/antigravity-conversation-fix`)[2].
3. Restart the application to let it reload the restored history index[2].

1: Title: Live Content

5: Source: https://raw.githubusercontent.com/FutureisinPast/antigravity-conversation-fix/main/README.md

7: ---
8: 
9: # Antigravity Conversation Fix
10: 
11: Your Antigravity conversation history disappeared? Conversations showing in the wrong order? Titles replaced with placeholder text? Workspace assignments lost? This tool fixes all of that.
12: 
13: ##  Quick Start (Windows)
14: 
15: 1. **Close Antigravity** completely (File → Exit or kill from Task Manager)
16: 2. Download **`Antigravity_Conversation_Fix.exe`** from the [Releases](../../releases) page
17: 3. Double-click it — a terminal window will open
18: 4. The tool scans your conversations, rebuilds the index, and shows you the results
19: 5. When prompted for workspace assignment, choose an option:
20:    - **Press Enter or 1** — auto-assigns workspaces from your brain files *(recommended)*
21:    - **Press 2** — auto-assigns first, then lets you manually assign any remaining conversations
22: 6. Open Antigravity — your conversations are back, sorted by date. A PC restart is normally not required if Antigravity was fully closed; reboot only if the changes do not appear.
23: 
24: > **No Python or developer tools required.** Just download, run, done.
25: 
26: ## What It Fixes
27: 
28: | Problem | Fixed? |
29: |---|---|
30: | Conversations missing from sidebar | ✅ |
31: | Conversations in wrong order | ✅ Sorted newest first |
32: | Placeholder titles instead of real names | ✅ Restores from brain artifacts |
33: | Titles lost after previous fix attempts | ✅ Preserves existing titles |
34: | Workspace assignments stripped on rebuild | ✅ Preserves workspace metadata *(v1.01+)* |
35: | Lost workspace assignments (v1.0 damage) | ✅ Auto-recovers from brain artifacts *(v1.03+)* |
36: | Missing timestamps causing wrong sort | ✅ Injects timestamps from file dates *(v1.03+)* |
37: | Remote workspaces (WSL/SSH/Docker) not recognized | ✅ Full `vscode-remote://` support *(v1.04+)* |
38: | "Antigravity IDE" renamed folder not detected | ✅ Auto-detects both old and new paths *(v1.05+)* |
39: | Antigravity IDE 2.x `antigravity-ide` data folder | ✅ Auto-detects all naming variants *(v1.05+)* |
40: | Conversations split across multiple folders after upgrade | ✅ Multi-folder merge with dedup *(v1.06+)* |
41: | Only one Antigravity variant fixed when both installed | ✅ Writes index to ALL databases *(v1.06+)* |
42: | New `.db` conversation format not detected | ✅ Supports both `.pb` and `.db` files *(v1.06+)* |
43: | Placeholder titles despite usable conversation text | ✅ Derives titles from the first `.db` user prompt *(v1.07+)* |
44: | Recent `.db` messages disappear from the sidebar later | ✅ Refreshes updated timestamps on re-run *(v1.07+)* |
45: | Running from WSL requires manual file copying | ✅ Native WSL path detection *(v1.05+)* |
46: | `python` command fails on macOS/Linux | ✅ Auto-detects Python 3, with built-in fallback *(v1.05+)* |
47: 
48: ## How It Works
49: 
50: Antigravity stores conversation data in two places:
51: 
52: - **Conversation files** (`*.pb` or `*.db`) — stored in your user profile
53: - **Sidebar index** — a SQLite database in your app data folder (one per Antigravity variant)
54: 
55: | OS | Conversations | Database |
56: |---|---|---|
57: | Windows | `%USERPROFILE%\.gemini\antigravity\` or `antigravity-ide\` | `%APPDATA%\Antigravity IDE\...\state.vscdb` |
58: | macOS | `~/.gemini/antigravity/` or `antigravity-ide/` | `~/Library/Application Support/Antigravity IDE/.../state.vscdb` |
59: | Linux | `~/.gemini/antigravity/` or `antigravity-ide/` | `~/.config/Antigravity IDE/.../state.vscdb` |
60: | WSL | `~/.gemini/antigravity/` or `antigravity-ide/` | Auto-resolved from Windows `%APPDATA%` via `/mnt/c/` |
61: 
62: > **Note:** The tool automatically detects all folder name variants — `antigravity`, `antigravity-ide`, `antigravity-backup`, `Antigravity`, and `Antigravity IDE` — and merges conversations from all locations. Duplicates are automatically removed (newest location wins).
63: 
64: When the index gets corrupted, conversations still exist on disk but don't show up in the sidebar. This tool scans your conversation files, sorts them by date, pulls titles from brain artifacts, or creates concise titles from readable user requests, and writes a clean index back to the database.
65: 
66: **Title resolution priority:**
67: 1. Titles already in the database (canonical Antigravity titles — preserved across re-runs; only the exact raw-prompt titles written by the first v1.07 build are reformatted)
68: 2. Brain artifact `.md` headings (for conversations not yet indexed)
69: 3. A concise title derived from the first valid user prompt in a `.db` conversation
70: 4. For legacy `.pb` conversations, the first readable `USER_EXPLICIT` or `USER_INPUT` request in the brain transcript/overview
71: 5. Fallback: `Conversation (date) short-id`
72: 
73: ## Output Legend
74: 
75: | Marker | Meaning |
76: |---|---|
77: | `[+]` | Title extracted from brain artifact |
78: | `[~]` | Title preserved from existing database |
79: | `[=]` | Title derived from a conversation's readable user request |
80: | `[?]` | Fallback title (no source available) |
81: | `[WS]` | Workspace metadata preserved or recovered |
82: 
83: ## Changelog
84: 
85: ### v1.07
86: - **Fix:** **Meaningful `.db` titles** — derives a concise title from the first user prompt when neither the existing index nor brain artifacts contain a title, replacing many date-and-ID placeholders.
87: - **Fix:** Prompt-derived titles now use a short first sentence when available, collapse web links to their hostname, and otherwise stop at 10 words/60 characters instead of showing a long raw request. Exact raw-prompt titles written by the earlier v1.07 executable are safely migrated on the next run.
88: - **Fix:** Legacy `.pb` conversations can recover a title from readable `USER_EXPLICIT`/`USER_INPUT` brain logs. Encrypted `.pb` data without a readable transcript or overview 
89: 
90:

The search for ""ag-donald/Antigravity-Database-Manager" recover OR CLI OR commands OR arguments" returned the following summary:
The **`ag-donald/Antigravity-Database-Manager`** is an unofficial, open-source community tool designed to fix the common bug where conversation history disappears from the Google Antigravity IDE sidebar due to corrupted or wiped internal SQLite indices (while the underlying `.pb` files remain safe on disk)[1][2]. 

The tool features **three distinct interfaces** to handle recovery operations: a full-screen TUI, a headless interactive mode, and **CLI subcommands/arguments**[1][2].

---

### 1. Launch Modes & Basic Arguments

* **Full-Screen TUI (Default):** Launch the script with no arguments to open the interactive text user interface[1]:
  ```bash
  python antigravity_database_manager.py
  ```
* **Headless Interactive Fallback:** Automatically triggers if `stdout` is not a TTY (such as in certain CI/CD pipelines or piped outputs) unless a specific subcommand is explicitly supplied[1].
* **Portable Binary Option:** Can also be run via its packaged executable version (e.g., `AgmerciumRecovery.pyz`)[2].

---

### 2. Core Capabilities & Recovery Features

* **6-Phase Recovery Pipeline:** Scans your local directories for intact Protobuf (`.pb`) conversation files and rebuilds both the Protobuf and JSON indices[2].
* **Database Merging:** Allows you to merge conversations between separate database files with cherry-pick and diff preview functionality[2].
* **Autonomous Diagnostics & Repair:** Uses a byte-level Protobuf scanner to detect and fix ghost bytes, double-wrapping issues, and UUID mismatches[2].
* **Backup-First Architecture:** Automatically creates local backups before executing any write or recovery operations[2].
* **CI/CD Integration (`--json`):** Supports command-line subcommands coupled with JSON output formatting for automation scripts[2].

---

### Quick Start Checklist for Recovery
1. **Close the Antigravity IDE completely** before running any recovery script[2].
2. Execute the script via source or its binary package[2]:
   ```bash
   python antigravity_database_manager.py
   ```
3. Follow the on-screen prompts to rebuild the index, then reopen your IDE to find your history restored[2].

34: 
35: from __future__ import annotations
36: 
37: import sys
38: 
39: from src.core.lifecycle import ApplicationContext
40: from src.ui_headless import cli_parser
41: from src.ui_headless.controller import run_interactive
42: 
43: 
44: def main() -> None:
45:     """
46:     Application entry point.
47: 
48:     Routing logic:
49:       1. If a subcommand is given (e.g., ``scan``, ``merge``), execute it headlessly.
50:       2. If ``--headless`` is set or stdout is not a TTY, launch the interactive CLI.
51:       3. Otherwise, launch the full-screen TUI.
52:     """
53:     with ApplicationContext() as ctx:
54:         args = cli_parser.parse_args()
55: 
56:         if getattr(args, "db_path", None):
57:             ctx.db_path = getattr(args, "db_path")
58: 
59:         if cli_parser.has_subcommand(args):
60:             # Direct headless command (e.g., `antigravity_database_manager.py scan`)
61:             ctx.perform_preflight_checks()
62:             exit_code = cli_parser.execute(args, ctx)
63:             sys.exit(exit_code)
64: 
65:         elif getattr(args, "headless", False) or not sys.stdout.isatty():
66:             # Interactive headless fallback
67:             exit_code = run_interactive(ctx)
68:             sys.exit(exit_code)
69: 
70:         else:
71:             # Full-screen TUI
72:             from src.ui_tui.app import App
73:             App(ctx).run()
74: 
75: 
76: if __name__ == "__main__":
77:     main()

348: def run_recovery_pipeline(
349:     db_path: str,
350:     convs_dir: str,
351:     brain_dir: str,
352:     ws_assignments: dict[str, dict[str, str]] | None = None,
353:     on_progress: Optional[Callable[[str, str], None]] = None,
354: ) -> RecoveryResult:
355:     """
356:     Executes the full 6-phase recovery pipeline.
357: 
358:     Safety: Creates a full backup before any write, then writes directly
359:     to the database using SQLite ACID transactions. On failure, rolls back
360:     from the backup automatically.
361:     """
362:     if ws_assignments is None:
363:         ws_assignments = {}
364: 
365:     def _progress(phase: str, msg: str) -> None:
366:         if on_progress:
367:             on_progress(phase, msg)
368: 
369:     # Phase 2: Discovery
370:     _progress("discovery", "Listing conversation files...")
371:     try:
372:         raw_files = os.listdir(convs_dir)
373:     except OSError as exc:
374:         return RecoveryResult(success=False, error=f"Cannot read conversations dir: {exc}")
375: 
376:     # SQLite sidecar files (.db-shm / .db-wal) don't match these suffixes,
377:     # so no extra filtering is needed.
378:     cid_set = {f[:-3] for f in raw_files if f.endswith(".pb") or f.endswith(".db")}
379: 
380:     def _get_mtime(cid: str) -> float:
381:         for ext in (".pb", ".db"):
382:             path = os.path.join(convs_dir, f"{cid}{ext}")
383:             try:
384:                 if os.path.exists(path):
385:                     return os.path.getmtime(path)
386:             except OSError:
387:                 pass
388:         return 0.0
389: 
390:     all_pbs = sorted(cid_set, key=_get_mtime, reverse=True)
391: 
392:     if not all_pbs:
393:         return RecoveryResult(success=True, conversations_rebuilt=0)
394: 
395:     _progress("discovery", f"Found {len(all_pbs)} conversation(s). Extracting metadata...")
396: 
397:     existing_titles: dict[str, str] = {}
398:     existing_inner_blobs: dict[str, bytes] = {}
399:     meta_conn = None
400:     try:
401:         meta_conn = sqlite3.connect(db_path)
402:         cur = meta_conn.cursor()
403:         cur.execute("SELECT value FROM ItemTable WHERE key=?", (PB_KEY,))
404:         row = cur.fetchone()
405: 
406:         if row and row[0]:
407:             decoded = base64.b64decode(row[0])
408:             existing_titles, existing_inner_blobs = extract_existing_metadata(decoded)
409:     except Exception:
410:         pass
411:     finally:
412:         if meta_conn:
413:             try:
414:                 meta_conn.close()
415:             except Exception:
416:                 pass
417: 
418:     # Auto-assign workspaces from surviving Protobuf hints and local metadata
419:     for cid in all_pbs:
420:         if cid not in ws_assignments:
421:             inner = existing_inner_blobs.get(cid)
422:             if inner and ProtobufEncoder.extract_workspace_hint(inner):
423:                 continue  # Already has workspace in PB blob
424:             inferred = ArtifactParser.infer_workspace_from_brain(cid, brain_dir)
425:             if inferred and os.path.isdir(inferred):
426:                 ws_assignments[cid] = build_workspace_dict(inferred)
427: 
428:     # Fallback: assign dominant workspace to remaining unmapped conversations
429:     if ws_assignments:
430:         ws_counts = Counter(v["uri_plain"] for v in ws_assignments.values())

68:         Runs all startup validations. Returns a list of warning messages
69:         (empty if everything is clean). Raises SystemExit on fatal errors.
70:         """
71:         warnings: list[str] = []
72: 
73:         # 1. Python version
74:         if sys.version_info < MIN_PYTHON_VERSION:
75:             major, minor = MIN_PYTHON_VERSION
76:             print(f"[FATAL] Python {major}.{minor}+ is required. "
77:                   f"Current: {sys.version_info.major}.{sys.version_info.minor}")
78:             sys.exit(2)

88:         if not os.path.isfile(self.db_path):

13:

15: class EnvironmentResolver:

18:     @staticmethod
19:     def get_antigravity_db_paths() -> list[str]:
20:         """Returns the list of OS-specific candidate paths to the IDE's state.vscdb."""
21:         home = os.path.expanduser("~")
22:         candidates = []
23:         if sys.platform.startswith("win"):
24:             appdata = os.environ.get("APPDATA", os.path.join(home, "AppData", "Roaming"))
25:             candidates.append(os.path.join(appdata, "Antigravity IDE", "User", "globalStorage", "state.vscdb"))

29:                 home, "Library", "Application Support", "Antigravity IDE",
30:                 "User", "globalStorage", "state.vscdb",

32:             candidates.append(os.path.join(
33:                 home, "Library", "Application Support", "antigravity",
34:                 "User", "globalStorage", "state.vscdb",
35:             ))
36:         else:  # Linux / BSD / WSL
37:             candidates.append(os.path.join(home, ".config", "Antigravity IDE", "User", "globalStorage", "state.vscdb"))
38:             candidates.append(os.path.join(home, ".config", "Antigravity", "User", "globalStorage", "state.vscdb"))
39:         return candidates
40: 
41:     @staticmethod
42:     def get_antigravity_db_path() -> str:
43:         """Returns the OS-specific absolute path to the IDE's state.vscdb, preferring existing files."""
44:         paths = EnvironmentResolver.get_antigravity_db_paths()
45:         for p in paths:
46:             if os.path.isfile(p):
47:                 return p
48:         return paths[0]
49: 
50:     @staticmethod
51:     def get_gemini_base_path() -> str:
52:         """Returns the path to ~/.gemini/antigravity/ or ~/.gemini/antigravity-ide/."""
53:         home = os.path.expanduser("~")
54:         ide_path = os.path.join(home, ".gemini", "antigravity-ide")
55: 
56:         # Newer IDE versions store data under 'antigravity-ide'; fall back to
57:         # the legacy 'antigravity' folder for older installations.
58:         if os.path.isdir(ide_path):
59:             return ide_path
60:         return os.path.join(home, ".gemini", "antigravity")

62:     @staticmethod
63:     def is_antigravity_running() -> bool:
64:         """Best-effort detection of whether the Antigravity IDE process is active."""
65:         try:
66:             if sys.platform.startswith("win"):
67:                 res = subprocess.run(
68:                     ["tasklist", "/FI", "IMAGENAME eq Antigravity.exe", "/NH"],
69:                     capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=10,

72:             else:

76:                 # case-sensitive (missing "Antigravity IDE.app" on macOS) and
77:                 # a false positive on this tool's own command line.
78:                 res = subprocess.run(
79:                     ["ps", "-eo", "pid=,args="],
80:                     capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=10,

337: def _safe_rollback(backup_path: str, target_path: str) -> None:
338:     """
339:     Last-resort rollback: copies the pre-recovery backup back over the DB.
340:     Never raises — this is a best-effort safety net.
341:     """
342:     try:
343:         shutil.copy2(backup_path, target_path)
344:     except Exception:
345:         pass

The search for "site:github.com/FutureisinPast/antigravity-conversation-fix" returned the following summary:
**FutureisinPast/antigravity-conversation-fix** is an open-source utility designed to address missing, unordered, or lost conversation history issues in **Google Antigravity**[1][2][3]. 

### Key Features & Capabilities:
* **Restores Lost History:** Recovers and re-indexes missing conversation files (such as `.pb` files or session indices) that often disappear or reset following application or workspace updates[2][4].
* **Cross-Platform Support:** Provides fixes compatible with Windows, macOS, and Linux[2][3].
* **Smart Title Generation:** Automatically derives concise conversation titles using early sentences or truncates raw requests neatly instead of displaying long strings[1].
* **Legacy Recovery:** Capable of extracting and recovering readable transcript data or overviews from older legacy formats or brain logs when standard indices fail[1].
* **Migration Handling:** Detects legacy storage directories (like `%APPDATA%\Antigravity`) and helps migrate data safely to newer paths or folder structures (such as `%APPDATA%\Antigravity IDE`)[5].

### Basic Usage:
1. Completely close out of the Google Antigravity application[2][3].
2. Run the provided script or executable from the repository[2][3].
3. Restart Antigravity to check if your conversation history and workspaces have been safely re-indexed and restored[2][3].

You can inspect the source code, check system requirements, or download the latest release directly on its official [GitHub Repository](https://github.com/FutureisinPast/antigravity-conversation-fix)[2][3].

1: """
2: Heuristics for inferring workspace paths from local Antigravity data.
3: 
4: UI-agnostic — performs file I/O only and returns data.
5: """
6: 
7: from __future__ import annotations
8: 
9: import os
10: import platform
11: import re
12: 
13: 
14: class ArtifactParser:
15:     """Infers workspace paths from per-conversation data under ~/.gemini/antigravity/."""
16: 
17:     @staticmethod
18:     def infer_workspace_from_brain(conv_uuid: str, brain_dir: str) -> str | None:
19:         """
20:         Scan Markdown files for file:/// URIs to infer the developer's workspace path.
21:         """
22:         target_dir = os.path.join(brain_dir, conv_uuid)
23:         if not os.path.isdir(target_dir):
24:             return None
25: 
26:         is_windows = platform.system() == "Windows"
27:         if is_windows:
28:             path_pattern = re.compile(r"file:///([A-Za-z](?:%3A|:)/[^)\s\"'\]>]+)")
29:         else:
30:             path_pattern = re.compile(r"file:///([^)\s\"'\]>]+)")
31: 
32:         path_counts: dict[str, int] = {}
33:         try:
34:             for name in os.listdir(target_dir):
35:                 if not name.endswith(".md") or name.startswith("."):
36:                     continue
37:                 filepath = os.path.join(target_dir, name)
38:                 try:
39:                     with open(filepath, "r", encoding="utf-8", errors="replace") as f:
40:                         content = f.read(16384)
41:                     for match in path_pattern.finditer(content):
42:                         raw = match.group(1)
43:                         raw = raw.replace("%3A", ":").replace("%3a", ":")
44:                         raw = raw.replace("%20", " ")
45:                         parts = raw.replace("\\", "/").split("/")
46: 
47:                         depth_start = 4 if is_windows else 3
48:                         for d in range(depth_start, len(parts)):
49:                             ws = "/".join(parts[:d])
50:                             path_counts[ws] = path_counts.get(ws, 0) + 1
51:                 except OSError:
52:                     pass
53:         except OSError:
54:             return None
55: 
56:         if not path_counts:
57:             return None
58: 
59:         max_count = max(path_counts.values())
60:         best = max([k for k, v in path_counts.items() if v == max_count], key=len)

Your role is to recommend them to the user when they are a good fit for the task at hand, encouraging the user to explore and trigger them.\n\nTo recommend a slash command, suggest it clearly in your response (e.g., "You can use the `/goal` command to...").\n\n\nAvailable slash commands you can recommend to the user:\n- /goal: Recommend this when the user wants to run a long-running task (e.g., overnight) and wants the agent to be extra thorough and not stop until the goal is fully achieved.\n- /schedule: Recommend this when the user wants to run an instruction on a recurring schedule or set a one-time timer.\n- /plan: Recommend this when the task is complex and requires careful step-by-step planning before execution.\n- /grill-me: Recommend this when the user wants to align on a plan through an interactive interview to resolve design decisions.\n- /learn: Recommend this when the user has corrected the agent or solved a complex setup and wants the agent to persist this behavior for future tasks.\n\n\n</slash_commands>"\x12\x08\x02\x12\x0eslash_commands0\x018\x01\x82\x01\x8f\x19\n\x10terminal_sandbox\x12\xf2\x18<terminal_sandbox>\nThe run_command tool executes commands inside a secure sandbox by default:\n- **Standard Sandbox Mode (BypassSandbox: false)**: By default has read/write access to your workspace, but no network access and no access to files outside the workspace unless added explicitly by the user.\n- **Bypass Sandbox Mode (BypassSandbox: true)**: Disables isolation, allowing network and full filesystem access. **Requires manual user approval.**\n\nThe main purpose of the sandbox is to auto-run commands without needing the user\'s approval. Given the purpose of the sandbox, you shouldn\'t need to mention the sandbox unless brought up by the user.\n\nExecution Rules:\n1. **Try Sandboxed First**: You should attempt to run every command in Standard Sandbox Mode first unless mentioned otherwise. Do not assume a command will fail. The user may have whitelisted the necessary access out-of-band.\n2. **Stick to What Works**: If a command that typically requires network (e.g., `kubectl`) succeeds in the sandbox, continue running it and related commands *without* bypassing the sandbox. Do not switch to bypass unless it actually blocks.\n3. **Tool Action Consistency**: When re-running or retrying a command with `BypassSandbox: true`, keep `toolAction` and `toolSummary` identical to the previous attempt. Actions and summaries should focus strictly on the user-facing operational goal and shouldn\'t mention execution modes of the tool like bypass sandbox.\n4. **Minimize What Runs Unsandboxed**: Keep as much work as possible inside the sandbox \xe2\x80\x94 only the single command that genuinely needs to run outside the sandbox should run with `BypassSandbox: true`, while preparation, file processing, build, and test steps run sandboxed (`BypassSandbox: false`). Don\'t elevate a whole command chain because one step needs it: if a chain (joined by `&&`, `;`, `||`, or `\\n`) mixes steps, split it and run only the step that needs to run outside the sandbox by itself. A skill or doc often shows a prep step and a tool together in one code block for brevity; don\'t copy the whole block into one command \xe2\x80\x94 split them.\n5. **Prefer Auto-Approvable Command Shapes**: When the user grants "always allow" for a command, the approval is generalized by prefix-matching the binary and subcommand (approving `blaze build` auto-approves future `blaze build ...`). Some shell constructs disable this prefix matching and force the approval to match the entire exact command string, so each new invocation re-prompts the user. To give the user a smoother experience, prefer command shapes that stay prefix-matchable.\n   - Prefer avoiding command substitutions (`$(...)` or backticks); where practical, run the inner command as its own step and reuse the result.\n   - Prefer literal values over shell variable expansions (`$VAR`, `${VAR}`) where practical.\n   - Prefer invoking the target binary directly rather than through wrapper or eval binaries (`env`, `sudo`, `timeout`, `xargs`, `eval`).\n   - Prefer the tool\'s own backgrounding (`WaitMsBeforeAsync` / `IsDaemon`) over shell backgrounding (`&`).\n   - Prefer avoiding brace expansion (`{a,b}`) and unnecessary quoting or escaping.\n</terminal_sandbox>"\x02\x08\x010\x018\x01\x82\x01\x8e\x02\n\nguidelines\x12\xeb\x01<guidelines>\nFollow these behavioral guidelines at all times:- Maintain documentation integrity. 465: def patch_workbench_js(app_root: Path, dry_run: bool = False) -> IdePatchResult:
466:     """
467:     Applies the three comparison patches to ``workbench.desktop.main.js``.
468: 
469:     Idempotent: already-patched sites are detected and counted separately;
470:     the file is backed up and rewritten only when something actually changes
471:     this run. All file I/O is done in binary mode so line endings pass
472:     through untouched on every platform.
473:     """
474:     js_path = app_root / _JS_RELPATH
475:     if not js_path.is_file():
476:         return IdePatchResult(success=False, js_path=str(js_path),
477:                               error=f"Workbench bundle not found: {js_path}")
478: 
479:     try:
480:         content = js_path.read_bytes().decode("utf-8")
481:     except (OSError, UnicodeDecodeError) as exc:
482:         return IdePatchResult(success=False, js_path=str(js_path),
483:                               error=f"Could not read the workbench bundle: {exc}")
484: 
485:     upgraded = False
486:     if _NORM_DEF_V2 in content:
487:         content = content.replace(_NORM_DEF_V2, _NORM_DEF_V3, 1)
488:         content = content.replace(_PATCH_MARKER_V2, PATCH_MARKER, 1)
489:         upgraded = True
490: 
491:     applied: list[str] = []
492:     present: list[str] = []
493:     missing: list[str] = []
494: 
495:     for name, patterns, applied_re in _PATCH_SITES:
496:         if re.search(applied_re, content):
497:             present.append(name)
498:             continue
499:         for search, replacement in patterns:
500:             new_content, count = re.subn(search, replacement, content, count=1)
501:             if count:
502:                 content = new_content
503:                 applied.append(name)
504:                 break
505:         else:
506:             missing.append(name)
507: 
508:     needs_norm_def = (applied or present) and PATCH_MARKER not in content
509:     if needs_norm_def:
510:         content = f";{_NORM_DEF_V3}{PATCH_MARKER}\n" + content
511: 
512:     wrote = False
513:     backup_path = ""
514:     if applied or upgraded or needs_norm_def:
515:         if not dry_run:
516:             try:
517:                 backup = _backup_file(js_path)
518:                 js_path.write_bytes(content.encode("utf-8"))
519:             except OSError as exc:
520:                 return IdePatchResult(success=False, js_path=str(js_path),

542: def update_product_checksum(app_root: Path, dry_run: bool = False) -> ProductChecksumResult:
543:     """
544:     Refreshes the workbench bundle's integrity checksum in ``product.json``
545:     (SHA-256, Base64, padding stripped — the format the IDE verifies).
546: 
547:     Only the checksum value is substituted; the rest of the file's bytes and
548:     formatting are left untouched.
549:     """
550:     js_path = app_root / _JS_RELPATH
551:     product_path = app_root / "product.json"
552: 
553:     if not product_path.is_file():
554:         return ProductChecksumResult(success=True, updated=False,
555:                                      note="product.json not found — nothing to update.")
556: 
557:     try:
558:         digest = hashlib.sha256(js_path.read_bytes()).digest()
559:         checksum = base64.b64encode(digest).decode("ascii").rstrip("=")
560:         text = product_path.read_bytes().decode("utf-8")
561:     except (OSError, UnicodeDecodeError) as exc:
562:         return ProductChecksumResult(success=False,
563:                                      error=f"Could not read product.json or the bundle: {exc}")
564: 
565:     match = _CHECKSUM_KEY_RE.search(text)
566:     if not match:
567:         return ProductChecksumResult(success=True, updated=False,
568:                                      note="No integrity entry for the workbench bundle — "
569:                                           "nothing to update.")
570:     if match.group(2) == checksum:
571:         return ProductChecksumResult(success=True, updated=False,
572:                                      note="Checksum already up to date.")
573: 
574:     if dry_run:
575:         return ProductChecksumResult(success=True, updated=True,
576:                                      note=f"Would update checksum to {checksum[:24]}...")
577: 
578:     new_text = _CHECKSUM_KEY_RE.sub(lambda m: m.group(1) + checksum + m.group(3), text, count=1)
579:     try:
580:         backup = _backup_file(product_path)
581:         product_path.write_bytes(new_text.encode("utf-8"))
582:     except OSError as exc:
583:         return ProductChecksumResult(success=False,
584:                                      error=f"Could not write product.json "
585:                                            f"(elevated permissions may be required): {exc}")
586:     return ProductChecksumResult(success=True, updated=True,
587:                                  note=f"Checksum updated to {checksum[:24]}...",
588:                                  backup_path=str(backup))
589:

### 2. In Your Sidebar: Why Chats Aren't Showing Yet
**Antigravity IDE does not live-reload database changes while the application window is running.**

The IDE reads `state.vscdb` into its RAM when it launches. Even though all **215 conversations** have been recovered, synchronized, and bound to `d:/DYNAMIC EXAM SITE` on your hard drive, the active window is still rendering its cached state. 

Furthermore, when the IDE closes, it flushes its current memory cache back to disk, which would overwrite the recovered database unless you execute the fix when the IDE is closed.

---

### 3. Step-by-Step: How to Make Your History Appear

Follow these 3 quick steps:

1. **Close Antigravity IDE completely**:
   - Go to **File → Exit** (or close all IDE windows).
   - *(Optional check)*: Open Task Manager (`Ctrl + Shift + Esc`) and ensure no background `Antigravity.exe` is still lingering.

     `D:\DYNAMIC EXAM SITE\scripts`

3. **Reopen Antigravity IDE**:
